Proof 1: If H is a subgroup of a finite group G, |H| divides |G| (Lagrange)

Statement: If H is a subgroup of a finite group G, |H| divides |G| (Lagrange)

Hypotheses: G finite, H subgroup

Conclusion: |H| divides |G|

Definitions: group, subgroup, order

Proof (Variation #1):

Step 1: Define left cosets aH

Step 2: Show cosets partition G

Step 3: Each coset has |H| elements

Step 4: |G| = [G:H]*|H|

ID: 861bf84888b3 | Status: UNFORMALIZED | Complexity: O(n)

Mutation test: Removing 'finite' fails for infinite index

Provenance: Variation #1 for theorem in domain Groups

Proof 2: For prime p | |G|, element of order p exists (Cauchy)

Statement: For prime p | |G|, element of order p exists (Cauchy)

Hypotheses: G finite, p prime, p|G

Step 3: Orbits size 1 or p

Step 5: Find non-identity fixed point

Proof 3: Every finite group has a composition series

Statement: Every finite group has a composition series

Hypotheses: G finite

Conclusion: composition series exists

Definitions: composition series, simple group, quotient

Proof (Variation #3):

Step 1: If G simple, trivial series G itself

Step 2: Else find normal 1 < N < G

Step 3: Apply to G/N recursively

Step 4: Thread series: G > N > ... > {e}

Proof 4: Every group G embeds in Sym(G) via regular representation

Statement: Every group G embeds in Sym(G) via regular representation

Hypotheses: G group

Conclusion: G -> Sym(G) is injective homomorphism

Definitions: embedding, regular rep, symmetric

Proof (Variation #4):

Step 1: Map g to sigma_g where sigma_g(h) = gh

Step 2: sigma_g is a bijection (left multiplication)

Step 3: sigma_{gh} = sigma_g * sigma_h (homomorphism)

Step 4: Kernel is trivial: sigma_g = id => g = e

ID: a250c27afceb | Status: UNFORMALIZED | Complexity: O(1)

Mutation test: No removable hypothesis - always true

Provenance: Variation #4 for theorem in domain Groups
